# Hot — last-session cache

Freshest working state; the first page `/pickup` reads. Rebuilt fresh in the 2026-07-16 vault rebuild (the old cache is archived).

## Most recent (2026-07-20): Booking system code review

- Completed a read-only end-to-end review of public and authenticated booking, pass administration, credit accounting, uploads, availability, Google Calendar synchronization, migrations, and tests.
- Confirmed `/book/c` fails because generated friendly slugs can be one character while both the edge function and RPC reject slugs shorter than three characters.
- Found P1 defects in manual-pass URLs, cancellation/calendar consistency, credit-refund concurrency, stale-calendar double booking, upload retries, final-credit Meet visibility, pending Google reconciliation, paused-pass resolution, and availability boundaries.
- Typecheck passed and all 10 booking-slot tests passed. The full suite still has the previously documented unrelated failures. No booking/API integration tests exist.

## 2026-09-08 (05:20 UTC) — RRC infrastructure audit: the 37-minute offset was an orphaned recorder plus an overwriting recovery

Root cause of Celia's clock offset, proven from the data: her assignment was opened at 23:28:48, the recorder that stamped her rows started at 00:06:27 (37 min 39 s later), her file uploaded at 01:12:34, and storage shows the object **updated at 01:30:49**. The first sitting's recorder was never stopped on unmount (only the finish path called `stop`), so it kept the microphone open for two hours and kept writing backup chunks; when she reloaded after finishing, the recovery pass took that orphan for a crashed run and uploaded its 116-minute file over her real 66-minute recording with `upsert: true`. Both sittings were Attempt 1 because the first wrote no rows, so both named the same path.

Fixed in the app, with red-then-green tests: the recorder hook closes both recorders and the mic tracks in its own unmount cleanup; the runner discards the backup of any run that unmounts without finishing (keyed on `saving`, because `finishing` is raised on every unmount); `discard` joins the per-run write chain and marks the run so a recorder's final chunk cannot resurrect it; recovery uploads with `upsert: false`, forgets a run on a 409, and tries newest sitting first. ADR 0034 written. Tests: 96 files / 2,674 passing, typecheck clean, one pre-existing lint warning.

Second victim found by the audit: **Charles** (moorecamooreca1@gmail.com) finished PT1.S3 at 21:03 UTC on 2026-09-07 (66 min, 64 MB) and sat "still being marked" for 8 hours: the old grader died on the file size and nothing ever retried, because the sweeper is unscheduled by design and the card gives up after 3 minutes. Re-driven at 05:16 UTC: 71% coverage, 16 of 22 coloured, no clock shift needed. New admin tool `scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts` lists every finished section older than 10 minutes with no complete Report Card, and `-- --grade` re-asks the grader for each. Run it daily until the sweeper is scheduled.

Still open, by design or decision: the sweeper stays unscheduled (ADR 0021), so a refused or lost grade reaches nobody unless the script runs; a run the grader refuses for good (no recording, clock it cannot place) shows "still being marked" forever with no message to Joshua; `send-section-report` fails `deno check` on a missing `@lovable.dev/email-js` dependency (MCQ pipeline, not RRC); Celia's stored recording is still the 116-minute orphan file (graded correctly through alignment, but the clips on her card seek into the shifted file).

## 2026-09-08 (09:30 UTC) — The Report Card becomes a page she can read, in colour that means something

Joshua's brief, in his words: highly organised, easy to follow and run through, high-end, click to hear her recording with the transcript highlighting as it plays against the golden standard, very specific critiques and very specific congratulations, spoken in his voice. Then three corrections as it went: **not cream** ("overdone and too AI looking"), **not emerald**, and **much more colourful than grey**. Green was a typo, not a request.

**Structure.** Twenty-two collapsible rows, each opening into ten stacked blocks of undifferentiated small text, became a ledger of twenty-two marks on a console plus exactly one stimulus open below it, in a fixed order she learns once: the passage, what you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time. Previous/Next walks the run without going back to the ledger.

**The transcript follows the sound.** The grader already stored `startMs`/`endMs` for every line she spoke, so the playhead alone drives the highlight; no word timings, no re-grade, and grades written before today get it too. Her recording and the standard's are one transport at the top rather than two text links at the foot of an expanded row. Line timings exist on 68 of 74 lines in Celia's row, and the six without simply get no play button.

**Specific congratulation.** "What you held" is read off measured facts and sits ahead of the correction. Before this a stimulus at the standard got one sentence and one at 86% got nothing, so twenty of twenty-two rows were a list of what went wrong.

**No Detail arithmetic on screen.** A Detail is our grading unit and a student has never seen one enumerated, so "20 of 21 details" is gone: `howMuch()` says "Almost all of it came back". Joshua's rule, and it applies to any future student-facing surface.

**Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity** — the mechanic is Grammarly's, and it is what stops twenty-two stimuli of corrections reading as a heat map of failure. Detail plum-rose, Read teal, what she brought of her own gold, method indigo; a miss is the colour of the thing it is a miss of. Magenta is the exception and means sound. Band stays carried by FILL not hue, so the ledger survives grayscale.

**Hues researched, not picked** (Firecrawl agents, server-side). Over-used in current award-winning work: acid yellow, electric cobalt, orange and coral, warm sand and terracotta, black plus one neon accent. Under-used: violet and plum, soft cyan, dusty rose as a field, cool neutrals. Orange-red was this file's first answer and is on the over-used list, so it moved. Neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is (`#fffbfe`, `#7d5260`), a system built to hold AA. A later job recommended blue for a generic "correct" token and warned gold reads as rank; it does not apply, because this card has no correct/incorrect token and gold is a category, not a verdict.

**`bigMisses` promoted.** The grader has returned at most three plain-words misses since it was written and the card printed them as one grey line at the foot of the line-by-line block. They now lead "What slipped", with the full missed list folded under. Every spoken-feedback product that does this well surfaces a small actionable set (Busuu names three).

**Verified:** 96 test files / 2,685 Activity Centre tests, typecheck clean, and every text node measured at 4.5:1 or better in a real browser across the default, playing and clean states (34 samples, 0 failures) via `scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs`, which converts computed colours through a canvas because Chrome serialises them as `oklch()`. Screenshots at 1180px and 390px via `scripts/_rc_shots.mjs`.

**New for future RRC report work:** `src/features/activity-centre/components/report-card.css` is the scoped world (`.rc`), the design decisions are written into its docstring and into `.impeccable/surfaces/src-features-activity-centre.md` under "The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)". `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html` renders the card at twenty-two stimuli **without `App.tsx`**, which is what made it workable while another session's CSS was mid-edit and 500ing the dev server: `?state=clean|faults|partial`, `?playing=N` to freeze the playhead.

Commits `3bd7aba`, `a9cb6e2`, both on origin/main. Discarded with Joshua's confirmation: an uncommitted local edit to `questionfinder/questionFinder.css` from another session; origin's `c8e4478` already carried the better fix (it hides the shortcut hint too).

**Correction to an earlier claim in this session:** I reported the "Every cylinder" branch as dead code because the judge never returns an empty `next_time` across 44 real stimuli. That was wrong. The card gates on measured criteria, not the empty string, so it fires correctly; Celia never triggers it because she gave no Read on any of the 22, which is the right answer. The narrower finding stands: the judge has never once left `next_time` empty.

Not done: no student-login browser check, so the playhead has never been seen against a real recording. No retry path from a miss, which the education research was consistent about. The `next_time` voice pass is still outstanding (11 em-dashes across 44 lines, "Push past just retelling" ten times) and needs a prompt change plus a deploy.

## 2026-09-08 (11:20 UTC) — Ivory could not open his drill, and nothing was watching

Two mails from Ivory Johnson III. Monday: "would you know when I should be able to see the assigned work on my portal?" Tuesday, with a screenshot: "Not open yet. This part of the classroom isn't in your plan right now."

**Not a broken link.** `has_activity_access` was `false` on his profile while `has_classroom_access` was `true`, which is why he could sign in and reach the classroom and it looked like the URL was at fault. One flag caused both mails: `/activities/:id` sits behind `ProtectedRoute flag="has_activity_access"`, and Classroom's own feed drops activity rows for anyone without it (`useClassroomHome.ts:189`), so his three RRC sections were invisible AND unreachable. He is the only one of nine students with RRC assigned who was missing it; his was a hand-granted subset (classroom, drill, chat, schedule) that missed activity. Turned it on at Joshua's go-ahead. There is no per-drill flag and there cannot be one, but the scoping was moot: `/activities` lists only a student's own assignments and he has zero non-RRC activities, so the flag opened exactly the three RRC sections and nothing else.

**The systemic fix.** `scripts/assign.ts` now grants the flag the assigned work needs, after the write, never before, granting nothing else and revoking nothing; it fails loudly rather than reporting a clean assign. `scripts/access-audit.ts` is the read-only sweep for work arriving by other paths (admin UI, Lovable-side writes, hand-run SQL) and names problems without fixing them, because opening a feature on a student's account is Joshua's call. Currently clean: 13 students with assigned work, all able to open it. Commit `15a5a2b`.

**Reply drafted, not sent:** Gmail draft in thread `1a07c3c954127a8c`, copy at `wiki/IVORY-REPLY-DRAFT-2026-09-08.md`. Owns it in the first line, names the cause, tells him the three sections and to re-sign-in if the page is stale.

**The lesson, which is the point of writing this down.** The product had no opinion about whether a student could open what it had just written to their account. Every route is gated and every feed filters, so a missing flag is not a locked door, it is an empty page: the failure is silent on both sides, and the only detector was a student polite enough to send a screenshot. When a surface is gated AND its feed filters on the same flag, assigning into it needs a guarantee at the write, not a hope. Next time work is assigned by any path, check the student can reach it in the same breath.

=== student-work usage ===
/**
 * What has a student actually done on LSAT U.
 *
 * The read half of the assign loop: `scripts/assign.ts` puts work into an
 * account, this reports back what came of it. Signs in as the admin and reads
 * under normal admin RLS — the same visibility the Teacher Desk has.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/student-work.ts -- --student "Celia"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/student-work.ts -- --student "Steven Morris" --days 30
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;

## What was written

- 25 transcripts exported into `students/<slug>/transcript-downloads/`, named
  `YYYY-MM-DD-<slug>-notes-by-gemini.md` to match the existing convention.

## What was deliberately not written

- `email-context.md`, `email-correspondence.md`, `payment-links.md` — these are
  mirrors of Gmail and Stripe and want their own pass. Inventing them would put
  unsourced content in files the README marks as precious.
- `teaching-notes.md` — durable judgments want a read of the full transcripts,
  not the summary lines.
- Target scores and test dates: not stated anywhere in the notes on record.

## Assigning

Use the `assign-lsatu-work` skill:

```bash
npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"
```

The script resolves a name or email to exactly one account and **refuses on
ambiguity** rather than guessing, because a misdirected assignment is silent —
she simply never sees it.

Full path and what it fires: [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]].

Tool result:
  const { error: authErr } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({
    email: env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,
    password: [REDACTED],
  });
  if (authErr) {
    console.error(`sign-in failed: ${authErr.message}`);
    process.exit(1);
  }

  const { data: users, error } = await supabase.functions.invoke("admin-manage-users", {
    method: "GET",
  });
  if (error) throw new Error(`admin-manage-users failed: ${error.message}`);

  const q = query!.trim().toLowerCase();
  const rows = (users ?? []) as { class_id: string; display_name: string | null; email: string | null }[];
  const exact = rows.filter((r) => r.email?.toLowerCase() === q || r.display_name?.toLowerCase() === q);
  const pool = exact.length
    ? exact
    : rows.filter((r) => r.display_name?.toLowerCase().includes(q) || r.email?.toLowerCase().includes(q));

  if (pool.length !== 1) {
    console.error(pool.length ? `"${query}" is ambiguous:` : `no student matches "${query}".`);
    for (const r of pool) console.error(`  - ${r.display_name ?? "(no name)"} <${r.email ?? "?"}>`);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  const s = pool[0];
  const id = s.class_id;

  console.log(`\n${s.display_name ?? "(no name)"}  <${s.email ?? "?"}>`);
  console.log(`last ${days} days\n${"─".repeat(60)}`);

  // Activities
  const { data: acts } = await supabase
    .from("activity_assignments")
    .select("id,title,status,mode,created_at")
    .eq("student_id", id)
    .order("created_at", { ascending: false });
  console.log(`\nACTIVITIES (${acts?.length ?? 0})`);
  for (const a of acts ?? []) {
    console.log(`  ${d(a.created_at)}  ${String(a.status).padEnd(12)} ${a.title}`);
  }

  // Read, Recall, Colour grades
  // There is no carried_pct column: the coverage figure lives inside the
  // `section` JSON as mean_coverage. Naming a column that does not exist makes
  // PostgREST answer 400, which this client surfaces as data: null, which read
  // for a week as "she has no graded runs". Hence the explicit error print.
  const { data: grades, error: gradeErr } = await supabase
    .from("activity_section_grades")
    .select("source_section,attempt_no,section,graded_at,created_at")
    .eq("student_id", id)
    .order("created_at", { ascending: false });
  if (gradeErr) console.log(`\nREAD, RECALL, COLOUR — could not read grades: ${gradeErr.message}`);
  if (grades?.length) {
    console.log(`\nREAD, RECALL, COLOUR — graded runs (${grades.length})`);
    for (const g of grades) {
      const s = (g.section ?? {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
      const cov = s.mean_coverage;
      const pct = cov == null ? "  —" : `${String(Math.round(Number(cov))).padStart(3)}%`;
      const done = s.complete === false ? "  (partial)" : "";
      const mins = s.total_seconds == null ? "" : `  ${Math.round(Number(s.total_seconds) / 60)} min`;
      console.log(`  ${d(g.graded_at ?? g.created_at)}  ${pct} coverage   ${g.source_section ?? "?"}  attempt ${g.attempt_no ?? "?"}${mins}${done}`);
    }
  }

  // Guided drills
  // The title is not on the assignment row — it lives on guided_drills, and the
  // date column is assigned_at, not created_at. Reading the wrong names here
  // returns a silent zero, which reads as "she has been given nothing".
  const { data: drills, error: drillErr } = await supabase
    .from("guided_drill_assignments")
    .select("status,assigned_at,completed_at,guided_drills(title)")
    .eq("student_id", id)
    .order("assigned_at", { ascending: false })
    .limit(15);
  if (drillErr) {
    console.log(`\nGUIDED DRILLS — could not read: ${drillErr.message}`);
  } else {
    console.log(`\nGUIDED DRILLS (${drills?.length ?? 0} most recent)`);
    for (const g of drills ?? []) {
      const title = (g as { guided_drills?: { title?: string } }).guided_drills?.title ?? "(untitled)";
      console.log(`  ${d(g.assigned_at)}  ${String(g.status).padEnd(12)} ${title}`);
    }
  }

  // Question-bank work
  const { count: attempts } = await supabase
    .from("attempts")
    .select("*", { count: "exact", head: true })
    .eq("user_id", id)

{"command": "cd C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu && cat > scripts/_empty_sweep_tmp.ts <<'EOF'\nimport fs from \"node:fs\"; import path from \"node:path\"; import { fileURLToPath } from \"node:url\";\nimport { supabase } from \"@/integrations/supabase/client\";\nconst REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), \"..\");\nfunction readEnv(f:string){const o:Record<string,string>={};if(!fs.existsSync(f))return o;for(const l of fs.readFileSync(f,\"utf8\").split(/\\r?\\n/)){const t=l.trim();if(!t||t.startsWith(\"#\"))continue;const i=t.indexOf(\"=\");if(i<0)continue;o[t.slice(0,i).trim()]=t.slice(i+1).trim().replace(/^[\"']|[\"']$/g,\"\");}return o;}\nconst env={...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO,\"../../.env\")),...readEnv(path.join(REPO,\".env\"))};\nconst { error } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({email:env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL,password:[REDACTED]});\nif(error){console.error(\"auth\",error.message);process.exit(1);}\nconst { data: users } = await supabase.functions.invoke(\"admin-manage-users\",{method:\"GET\"});\nconst byId=new Map((users as any[]??[]).map(u=>[u.class_id,u]));\nconst { data: all, error: e2 } = await supabase.from(\"activity_assignments\")\n  .select(\"id,title,status,item_count,source_section,student_id,created_at\").order(\"created_at\",{ascending:false});\nif(e2){console.error(e2.message);process.exit(1);}\nconst bad=(all??[]).filter(r=>!r.item_count || !r.source_section);\nconsole.log(`total assignments: ${all?.length}; empty/sectionless: ${bad.length}`);\nfor(const r of bad){const u=byId.get(r.student_id) as any;console.log(`  ${r.created_at.slice(0,10)} ${String(r.status).padEnd(10)} items=${r.item_count} sect=${r.source_section} ${u?.display_name??r.student_id} <${u?.email??\"?\"}> id=${r.id} | ${r.title}`);}\nprocess.exit(0);\nEOF\nnpx vite-node scripts/_empty_sweep_tmp.ts 2>&1 | tail -30", "description": "Sweep for empty assignments platform-wide", "timeout": 240000}

## user

Tool result:
  // "Activity not found." was shown for two very different situations: an id
  // that is not real, and an assignment that IS real but was issued to another
  // account. The second is the common one for anyone signed in as admin while
  // the assignment sits on their student login, and the old wording sent the
  // reader looking for a routing or RLS bug that was never there.
  // Tri-state, not a boolean. A boolean starting false means the first paint
  // after an empty load says "Activity not found." and only corrects itself
  // once the probe answers, so the wrong sentence is shown first and the right
  // one arrives as a flicker. "unknown" keeps the spinner up instead.
  const [ownership, setOwnership] = useState<"unknown" | "elsewhere" | "absent">("unknown");
  const { assignment, loading, error } = useStudentActivity(assignmentId, previewActivityId);

  /**
   * Joshua, opening a link he handed to a student.
   *
   * An admin can read every assignment, so the row loads; what must not happen
   * is his visit being recorded as the student's work. Every write below is
   * gated on `readOnly` rather than `practice` alone, and the attempt log is
   * read under the assignment's OWN student id, so what he sees on the screen
   * is their answers rather than an empty run.
   */
  const viewingSomeoneElse = Boolean(assignment && user && assignment.student_id !== user.id);
  const logStudentId = assignment?.student_id ?? user?.id;

  // Only asked when the load has genuinely come back empty, so the ordinary
  // path costs nothing. The probe reads by id alone and returns a boolean, so
  // a student who cannot see the row keeps the plain not-found wording.
  useEffect(() => {
    if (loading || error || assignment) {
      setOwnership("unknown");
      return;
    }
    // No id to probe (teacher preview, or a malformed route) can never resolve
    // to "belongs to someone else", and leaving it "unknown" would hold the
    // spinner up forever. Settle it here rather than waiting on a call that is
    // never made.
    if (!assignmentId) {
      setOwnership("absent");
      return;
    }
    let live = true;
    [startPractice, redrillOrders],
  );

  /** The ledger's re-drill: the misses, ungraded, in their original order. */
  const handleRedrill = useCallback(
    (orders: number[]) => startPractice(orders),
    [startPractice],
  );

  // The empty-load case is still resolving until the ownership probe answers,
  // so it waits here rather than showing a sentence it may have to take back.
  if (loading || (assignment && !hydrated) || (!error && !assignment && ownership === "unknown")) {
    return (
      <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen flex items-center justify-center">
        <div className="h-6 w-6 animate-spin rounded-full border-2 border-[hsl(var(--muted))] border-t-[hsl(var(--ac-emerald))]" />
      </div>
    );
  }

  if (error || !assignment) {
    return (
      <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen flex flex-col items-center justify-center gap-4 p-6 text-center">
        <p className="text-sm text-muted-foreground">
          {error
            ? `Could not load this activity: ${error}`
            : ownership === "elsewhere"
              ? "This activity was assigned to a different account."
              : "Activity not found."}
        </p>
        {!error && user?.email && (
          <p className="text-xs text-muted-foreground/70">
            {ownership === "elsewhere"
              ? `You are signed in as ${user.email}. Sign in as the account it was assigned to.`
              : `Signed in as ${user.email}.`}
          </p>
        )}

it.standard;\n      if(!lines || (Array.isArray(lines)&&lines.length===0)) probs.push(`item ${i+1}: no answer-key lines`);\n    });\n    const orders = items.map((it:any)=>it.order ?? it.item_order).filter((x:any)=>x!=null);\n    const dupes = orders.length!==new Set(orders).size;\n    console.log(`\\n-- ${r.title}  [${r.source_section}] items=${r.item_count} actual=${items.length} fmt=${r.item_format}`);\n    console.log(`   item keys: ${[...keys].join(\",\")}`);\n    console.log(`   orders ok: ${orders.length===items.length && !dupes}  goal: ${JSON.stringify(r.time_goal_minutes)}  instructions: ${r.instructions?Object.keys(r.instructions).join(\"/\"):\"NONE\"}`);\n    console.log(probs.length?`   PROBLEMS (${probs.length}): ${probs.slice(0,6).join(\"; \")}`:\"   no structural problems\");\n  }\n}\nprocess.exit(0);\nEOF\nnpx vite-node scripts/_items_check_tmp.ts 2>&1 | tail -80", "description": "Deep-check assignment item payloads", "timeout": 240000}

import { useCallback, useEffect, useMemo, useState } from "react";
import {
  listMySectionGrades,
  listSectionChoices,
  startSectionAttempt,
  type SectionChoice,
} from "../api/activitiesClient";
import type { ActivityAssignmentSummary, SectionGradeStub } from "../api/types";

/**
 * The sections she can run, and what she has already done.
 *
 * Read, Recall, Colour is one Activity now. Joshua assigns the drill; she picks
 * which section to work tonight, and she works several. That is why this list
 * exists at all: a section per library card meant he chose for her, and it grew
 * by one card every time a section was built.
 *
 * Two rules the drill puts on this screen:
 *
 * - **Nothing is hidden once done.** The regular section picker drops a section
 *   she has sat, which is right when there are two hundred of them and wrong
 *   here, where there are three. Repeating a section is legitimate: she is
 *   training recall, not measuring a score.
 * - **Every Attempt is kept.** Not a count, not the latest. Attempt 1, 2 and 3
 *   each keep their own date and their own Report Card, because the point of
 *   running a section twice is seeing what changed.
 *
 * Starting is a server call, not an insert. She chooses the section; the row
 * that holds her run is built under definer rights, because the snapshot
 * carries the Details her Coverage is scored against and must never be hers to
 * write.
 */
export interface SectionLibraryProps {
  /** Her assignments, used to show what she has already attempted. */
  attempts: ActivityAssignmentSummary[];
  /** Called with the new assignment id once an Attempt has been created. */
  onStart: (assignmentId: string) => void;
  /**
   * Called with the assignment of an Attempt she wants to look at again.
   *
   * Its own Report Card is what opens: a completed section routes there rather
   * than to the generic summary, from any device and at any later date.
   */
  onOpenAttempt?: (assignmentId: string) => void;
  /** Injected in tests. */
  loadChoices?: () => Promise<SectionChoice[]>;
  loadGrades?: () => Promise<SectionGradeStub[]>;
  beginAttempt?: (activityId: string) => Promise<string>;
}

function attemptDate(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary): string {
  const when = new Date(a.completed_at ?? a.created_at);
  return when.toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: "numeric", month: "short" });
}

/**
 * What one past Attempt says about itself.
 *
 * Three states, and they are genuinely different: a run that was never
 * finished, a finished one still being marked, and a marked one with a number.

=== ActivitySections page ===
import { useNavigate } from "react-router-dom";
import { SectionLibraryPanel } from "@/features/activity-centre/components/SectionLibraryPanel";
import "@/features/activity-centre/activity-centre.css";

/**
 * Read, Recall, Colour, reachable without waiting to be assigned it.
 *
 * The door used to exist only as an assignment: a student who had never been
 * given the section-library Activity had no address for it at all, even though
 * every section behind it is open to her and starting one is a server call that
 * builds its own Attempt. This is that same door at a permanent address, linked
 * from the Activities page.
 */
export default function ActivitySections() {
  const navigate = useNavigate();
  return <SectionLibraryPanel onExit={() => navigate("/activities")} />;
}

  /*
   * The door. Joshua assigns Read, Recall, Colour; she picks which section to
   * run tonight, and she runs several. This Activity has no items of its own:
   * its content is the list.
   *
   * It sits ABOVE the `phase === "intro"` return rather than below it, because
   * the door has no intro to precede it -- it IS the first screen. Behind that
   * return it was unreachable except by pressing Start on an editorial page
   * written for the typed Activities, which is how a student met three screens
   * before her first run and only the last of them was designed for this drill.
   */
  if (assignment.mode === "section-library") {
    return (
      <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen" data-runner="section-library">
        {adminBanner}
        <SectionLibraryPanel
          title={assignment.title}
          subtitle={assignment.subtitle}
          instructions={assignment.instructions}
          onExit={handleLeave}
        />
      </div>
    );
  }

  /*
   * A section's front door is its instructions, in full, on their own page.
   *
   * The editorial intro folded the method into tabs, so a student pressing
   * Start from the library either met a page she had to unfold or -- when the
   * launch skipped it -- a live microphone with no explanation at all.
   */
  if (phase === "intro" && assignment.mode === "section" && assignment.item_format === "spoken-recall") {
    return (
      <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen" data-runner="section-briefing">
        {adminBanner}
        <SectionBriefingPage
          title={assignment.title}
          subtitle={assignment.subtitle}
          instructions={assignment.instructions}
          onStart={handleStart}
          onExit={handleLeave}
        />
      </div>
    );

       * means the real recording landed; the pieces are surplus and go.
       */
      const { error } = await supabase.storage
        .from(run.bucket)
        .upload(run.path, blob, { contentType: run.mimeType, upsert: false });
      if (error) {
        if (alreadyStored(error)) {
          await discard(run.runId);
          continue;
        }
        // Keep it. The next open tries again, and until then this copy is the
        // only one that exists.
        console.error("[rrc] could not upload a recovered recording", error);
        continue;
      }

      await discard(run.runId);
      recovered += 1;
    }
  } catch (e) {
    console.error("[rrc] recovery pass failed", e);
  }
  return recovered;
}

## What I verified green

- **Nothing stuck.** `rrc-ungraded.ts`: every finished section older than 10 minutes has a complete Report Card (6 checked).
- **Celia's orphaned-recorder bug is deployed**, not just committed. Commit `9098226` is on origin/main and I found its fingerprint in the live prod chunk `SectionLibraryPanel-CjFeHEQD.js` — `upsert:!1` plus the 409 "already exists" branch. So no repeat of a stale recorder overwriting a finished run.
- 98 test files / 2,694 Activity Centre tests pass; the suite is green.

Yahunathan is the only one of the four RRC students **without the `section-library` "door" row** (Ore, Charles and Celia all have it). /**
 * Headless assign: put an Activity, a guided drill, or a plain message into a
 * student's LSAT U account from the command line.
 *
 * Run with vite-node so `@/` resolves and this calls the app's OWN assignWork()
 * rather than a second implementation of it. That matters: assigning touches
 * seven tables (assignment snapshot, classroom item, conversation, message,
 * notification, plus the email edge function) and rolls back on failure. A
 * reimplementation would drift from the UI the first time the app changed.
 *
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "Read, Recall, Colour"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --drill "main-conclusion-1"
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --message "Start with PT105.S2."
 *   npx vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student "Celia" --activity "..." --dry-run
 *
 * Credentials are read from ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored), never from this repo's
 * .env, which IS tracked in git. Same convention as scripts/db-pull.mjs.
 */
import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
import { supabase } from "@/integrations/supabase/client";
import { assignWork } from "@/lib/assignWork";

const REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), "..");

function readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {
  const out: Record<string, string> = {};
  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;
  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, "utf8").split(/\r?\n/)) {
    const t = line.trim();
    if (!t || t.startsWith("#")) continue;
    const i = t.indexOf("=");
    if (i < 0) continue;
    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^["']|["']$/g, "");
  }
  return out;
}

const env = {
  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, "../../.env")),
  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, ".env")),
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------- arguments

function arg(name: string): string | undefined {
  const i = process.argv.indexOf(`--${name}`);
  return i >= 0 ? process.argv[i + 1] : undefined;
}
const has = (name: string) => process.argv.includes(`--${name}`);

const studentQuery = arg("student");
const activityQuery = arg("activity");
const drillQuery = arg("drill");
const messageText = arg("message");
const scheduleFor = arg("schedule");
const dryRun = has("dry-run");

if (!studentQuery || (!activityQuery && !drillQuery && !messageText)) {
  console.error(
    "usage: vite-node scripts/assign.ts -- --student <name|email> \\\n" +
      "         (--activity <title> | --drill <slug|title> | --message <text>)\\\n" +
      "         [--schedule YYYY-MM-DD] [--dry-run]",
  );
  process.exit(2);
}
if (scheduleFor && !/^\d{4}-\d{2}-\d{2}$/.test(scheduleFor)) {
  console.error(`--schedule must be YYYY-MM-DD, got "${scheduleFor}"`);
  process.exit(2);

Tool result:
 * that predates the decision, it costs nothing to keep, and deleting a row to
 * fix a listing is a trade nobody should make. Assigning something unrunnable
 * is the actual harm, and this is where that is prevented.
 */
export function isRunnable(activity: Activity): boolean {
  if (activity.item_format !== "spoken-recall") return true;
  // A `section` row is content now, not something to assign: it is one of the
  // sections she picks from inside the library. The library is the door, and
  // the door is the only Read, Recall, Colour card in the list.
  return activity.mode === "section-library";
}

/** One row of the section list a student chooses from. */
export interface SectionChoice {
  id: string;
  title: string;
  source_section: string;
  stimulus_count: number;
  goal_minutes: Record<string, number>;
}

/**
 * The sections she may run.
 *
 * Read through a definer function rather than a select, because only admins may
 * read `activities` and the fix must not be a read policy: `items` carries the
 * Details her Coverage is scored against and the captured answer key, and a
 * policy cannot hand back some columns while withholding others.
 */
export async function listSectionChoices(): Promise<SectionChoice[]> {
  // Cast until `types.ts` is regenerated against the deployed schema. Both
  // functions arrived with 20260902200000 and the generated union predates it;
  // delete the casts the next time types are pulled.
  const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc("list_section_activities" as never);
  if (error) throw error;
  return (data ?? []) as unknown as SectionChoice[];
}

/**
 * Start an Attempt on one section, and return the assignment id to run.
 *
 * The row is built server-side. She chooses which section; she never supplies
 * what is in it. An abandoned Attempt on the same section is discarded first,
 * because the drill is one unbroken recording and half a run has no valid audio
 * to resume into.
 */
export async function startSectionAttempt(activityId: string): Promise<string> {
  const { data, error } = await supabase.rpc("start_section_attempt" as never, {
    p_activity_id: activityId,
  } as never);
  if (error) throw error;
  if (typeof data !== "string") throw new Error("start_section_attempt returned no id");
  return data;
}

/**
 * Start a focus set: the weakest stimuli of a finished run, as their own
 * Attempt.
 *
 * The row is built server-side from the run she already owns; the slugs only
 * narrow her own snapshot and can never widen it.
 */

  assignmentId: string,

Tool result:
create or replace function public.start_section_attempt(p_activity_id uuid)
returns uuid
language plpgsql
security definer
set search_path = public
as $fn$
declare
  v_student uuid := auth.uid();
  v_activity public.activities%rowtype;
  v_id uuid;
begin
  if v_student is null then
    raise exception 'not signed in';
  end if;

  select * into v_activity from public.activities where id = p_activity_id;
  if not found or v_activity.mode is distinct from 'section' then
    raise exception 'not a Read, Recall, Colour section';
  end if;

  -- An abandoned run is discarded rather than resumed (ADR 0023). The drill is
  -- one unbroken recording, so a half-run has no valid audio and nothing
  -- gradeable, and leaving it in the way would lock her out of the section she
  -- chose. A run that wrote rows is kept, as `abandoned`: the rows are what
  -- makes its recording recoverable, and the status is what keeps the grader
  -- and the sweeper off it. It used to be marked `completed` here, which is
  -- precisely the state both of them grade.
  update public.activity_assignments a
     set status = 'abandoned'
   where a.student_id = v_student and a.activity_id = p_activity_id
     and a.status in ('assigned', 'in_progress')
     and exists (select 1 from public.activity_item_responses r where r.assignment_id = a.id);

  delete from public.activity_assignments a
   where a.student_id = v_student and a.activity_id = p_activity_id
     and a.status in ('assigned', 'in_progress')
     and not exists (select 1 from public.activity_item_responses r where r.assignment_id = a.id);

  insert into public.activity_assignments
    (activity_id, student_id, class_id, title, subtitle, instructions, item_format,
     items, status, started_at, assigned_by, mode, time_goal_minutes, source_section)
  values
    (v_activity.id, v_student, v_activity.class_id, v_activity.title, v_activity.subtitle,
     v_activity.instructions, v_activity.item_format, v_activity.items, 'in_progress', now(),
     v_activity.created_by, v_activity.mode, v_activity.time_goal_minutes, v_activity.source_section)
  returning id into v_id;

  return v_id;
end;
$fn$;

}: SectionLibraryProps) {
  const [choices, setChoices] = useState<SectionChoice[] | null>(null);
  /**
   * Her Report Cards, by assignment.
   *
   * Loaded beside the sections and never waited on: the list is useful without
   * them, and a failed read costs a number on a row rather than the screen.
   */
  const [grades, setGrades] = useState<Map<string, SectionGradeStub>>(new Map());
  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [starting, setStarting] = useState<string | null>(null);

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    void (async () => {
      try {
        const rows = await loadChoices();
        if (!cancelled) setChoices(rows);
      } catch {
        // Named plainly. An empty list and a failed read are different facts,
        // and only one of them means "nothing to do tonight".
        if (!cancelled) setError("Could not load the sections. Try again in a moment.");
      }
    })();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, [loadChoices]);

  useEffect(() => {
    let cancelled = false;
    void (async () => {
      try {
        const rows = await loadGrades();
        if (cancelled) return;
        // Newest Attempt per assignment. `listMySectionGrades` returns them
        // newest first, so the first one seen for an assignment wins.
        const byAssignment = new Map<string, SectionGradeStub>();
        for (const g of rows) if (!byAssignment.has(g.assignment_id)) byAssignment.set(g.assignment_id, g);
        setGrades(byAssignment);
      } catch {
        // A missing number is a missing number. The list still works.
      }
    })();
    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
  }, [loadGrades]);

  /** Her attempts, newest first, grouped by which section they were on. */
  const bySection = useMemo(() => {
    const map = new Map<string, ActivityAssignmentSummary[]>();
    for (const a of attempts) {
      if (a.mode !== "section" || !a.source_section) continue;
      const list = map.get(a.source_section) ?? [];
      list.push(a);
      map.set(a.source_section, list);
    }
    for (const list of map.values()) {
      list.sort((x, y) => new Date(y.created_at).getTime() - new Date(x.created_at).getTime());
    }
    return map;
  }, [attempts]);

  const start = useCallback(
    async (choice: SectionChoice) => {
      setStarting(choice.id);
      setError(null);
      try {
        onStart(await beginAttempt(choice.id));
      } catch {
        setError("Could not start that section. Try again in a moment.");
        setStarting(null);
      }
    },
    [beginAttempt, onStart],
  );

  if (error && !choices) {
    return <p className="sl-note">{error}</p>;
  }

/**
 * What one past Attempt says about itself.
 *
 * Three states, and they are genuinely different: a run that was never
 * finished, a finished one still being marked, and a marked one with a number.
 * The old list said "done" for all three and led nowhere, so an Attempt she had
 * spent twenty-five minutes on was a line of grey text.
 */
function attemptLabel(a: ActivityAssignmentSummary, grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined): string {
  const date = attemptDate(a);
  if (a.status !== "completed") return `${date} · not finished`;
  if (!grade) return `${date} · still being marked`;
  return `${date} · ${grade.section.mean_coverage}% carried`;
}

/**
 * What the row is FOR, said on the row.
 *
 * Celia, on the morning after her first graded section: "I see the percentage,
 * but I'm not seeing a way to look back at the attempt to see what information
 * I was missing in my responses." The Report Card was already at the end of
 * this button and had been for weeks. The row simply never said so. It was
 * 13.5px muted text with a dotted underline, which is what a caption looks
 * like, and the label stopped at the number.
 *
 * A percentage is a verdict she cannot act on. The card behind it is the whole
 * point of the drill, so the row names it.
 */
function attemptAction(
  a: ActivityAssignmentSummary,
  grade: SectionGradeStub | undefined,
): string | null {
  if (a.status !== "completed") return null;
  if (!grade) return "Check back shortly";
  return "See what you missed";
}

export function SectionLibrary({
  attempts,
  onStart,
=== render ===
  return (
    <div className="sl-sections">
      {error && (
        <p className="sl-note" data-tone="bad">
          {error}
        </p>
      )}
      {choices.map((choice) => {
        const done = bySection.get(choice.source_section) ?? [];
        return (
          <div key={choice.id} className="sl-section" data-section={choice.source_section}>
            <div className="sl-sec-body">
              <div className="sl-sec-line">
                <span className="sl-sec-name">{choice.source_section}</span>
                {/*
                  * The time, not the stimulus count.
                  *
                  * A stimulus is our grain, not hers, and the counts differ per
                  * section for a reason she has no way to see: two questions can
                  * share a stimulus. Printed on a row to choose from, "22" next
                  * to "26" reads as one section being longer, which is not what
                  * it means. The clock is the thing she actually plans around.
                  */}
                <span className="sl-sec-clock">
                  {choice.goal_minutes?.standard ?? 25} min
                </span>
              </div>

              {done.length === 0 ? (
                <p className="sl-note">Not run yet.</p>
              ) : (
                /*
                 * Every Attempt, and every one of them a way back in.
                 *
                 * The Report Card used to exist only in the tab that produced it:
                 * this list rendered the same runs as plain text, so a grade she
                 * could not have seen -- grading finishes minutes after she stops
                 * -- was unreachable for ever after. A finished Attempt opens on
                 * its own Report Card; an unfinished one is not a run and does
                 * not pretend to lead anywhere.
                 */
                <ul className="sl-sec-attempts">
                  {done.map((a, i) => {
                    const grade = grades.get(a.id);
                    const label = `Attempt ${done.length - i} \u00b7 ${attemptLabel(a, grade)}`;
                    const action = attemptAction(a, grade);
                    const reachable = a.status === "completed" && onOpenAttempt !== undefined;
                    return (
                      <li key={a.id}>
                        {reachable ? (
                          <button
                            type="button"
                            className="sl-sec-attempt"
                            onClick={() => onOpenAttempt?.(a.id)}
                          >
                            <span className="sl-sec-attempt-label">{label}</span>
                            {action && <span className="sl-sec-attempt-go">{action}</span>}
                          </button>
                        ) : (
                          <span className="sl-sec-attempt" data-flat="true">
                            <span className="sl-sec-attempt-label">{label}</span>
                            {action && <span className="sl-sec-attempt-go">{action}</span>}
                          </span>
                        )}
                      </li>
                    );
                  })}
                </ul>
              )}
            </div>

            <button
              type="button"
              className="sl-sec-go"
              disabled={starting !== null}
              onClick={() => void start(choice)}
            >
              {starting === choice.id ? "Starting\u2026" : done.length > 0 ? "Run it again" : "Start"}
            </button>
          </div>
        );
      })}
    </div>
  );
}

import { describe, expect, it, vi } from "vitest";
import { fireEvent, render, screen } from "@testing-library/react";
import type { ActivityAssignmentSummary } from "../api/types";
import { SectionLibrary } from "./SectionLibrary";

// The list she picks a section from. Three rules the drill puts on it, each of
// which fails quietly rather than loudly if it breaks.

const choices = [
  { id: "a1", title: "…PT102 Section 3", source_section: "PT102.S3", stimulus_count: 22, goal_minutes: { standard: 25 } },
  { id: "a2", title: "…PT105 Section 2", source_section: "PT105.S2", stimulus_count: 23, goal_minutes: { standard: 25 } },
];

const attempt = (over: Partial<ActivityAssignmentSummary>): ActivityAssignmentSummary =>
  ({
    id: "x", student_id: "s", title: "t", status: "completed", item_count: 23,
    created_at: "2026-09-01T10:00:00Z", completed_at: "2026-09-01T10:30:00Z",
    mode: "section", source_section: "PT105.S2", instructions: {},
    ...over,
  }) as ActivityAssignmentSummary;

const load = () => Promise.resolve(choices);
/** No Report Cards unless a test says otherwise; the list works without them. */
const noGrades = () => Promise.resolve([]);

describe("choosing a section", () => {
  it("lists every section and never hides one she has done", async () => {
    // The regular picker drops a section she has sat. Right at 200 sections,
    // wrong at three: she would run out in three nights.
    render(<SectionLibrary attempts={[attempt({})]} onStart={vi.fn()} loadChoices={load} loadGrades={noGrades} />);
    await screen.findByText("PT102.S3");
    expect(screen.getByText("PT105.S2")).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("shows the clock and not the stimulus count", async () => {
    // A stimulus is our grain, not hers, and the counts differ per section for
    // a reason she cannot see: two questions can share a stimulus.
    render(<SectionLibrary attempts={[]} onStart={vi.fn()} loadChoices={load} loadGrades={noGrades} />);
    await screen.findByText("PT102.S3");
    expect(screen.getAllByText("25 min").length).toBe(2);
    expect(screen.queryByText(/22 stimuli/)).not.toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.queryByText(/stimuli/)).not.toBeInTheDocument();
  });

  it("lists every Attempt separately, not a count", async () => {
    render(
      <SectionLibrary
        attempts={[
          attempt({ id: "r1", created_at: "2026-09-01T10:00:00Z" }),
          attempt({ id: "r2", created_at: "2026-08-30T10:00:00Z" }),
        ]}
        onStart={vi.fn()}
        loadChoices={load}
        loadGrades={noGrades}
      />,
    );
    await screen.findByText("PT105.S2");
    expect(screen.getByText(/Attempt 2/)).toBeInTheDocument();
    expect(screen.getByText(/Attempt 1/)).toBeInTheDocument();
  });

The library read\nevery one of them as a run: it printed \"Attempt 1 - not finished\" for sections\nhe had never opened, offered \"Run it again\", and pressing that called\nstart_section_attempt, which DELETES a never-started row for the same section\nbefore inserting its own. ---
Read, Recall, Colour
01 · READ IT
0:01
STIMULUS

City planners argue that widening Elm Street will ease congestion. But in the three cities that widened comparable roads, traffic rose by roughly a fifth within two years. So widening Elm Street will probably make congestion worse, not better.

space
when you finish the piece
OUT LOUD

Reading silently. Nothing comes out yet.

DEMONSTRATION. THE STIMULUS AND THE TRANSCRIPT ARE WRITTEN FOR THIS PAGE.

LIBRARY/OUT LOUD
READ, RECALL, COLOUR
Read, Recall, Colour

Read it, tell it back, react to it. Then the stimulus disappears and you say the whole argument back from memory.

Choose a section
Read the full instructions
YOUR SECTIONS
PT105.S2
25 min
Attempt 2 · Sep 2 · 91% carried
See what you missed
Attempt 1 · Aug 31 · still being marked
Check back shortly
Run it again
PT102.S3
25 min
Attempt 1 · Sep 1 · not finished
Run it again
PT1.S3
25 min

Not run yet.

Start
BEFORE YOU START

Read each piece silently. Then look away and say it back in your own words. Give your reaction out loud too. After the final piece, recall the whole argument out loud from memory. Your entire section is recorded in one take, and you cannot pause the recording.

Every completed attempt stays here. If you run a section again, it is saved as a new attempt. Your earlier attempts are never replaced.

STEP 1
Read it.
Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.
in your own words
STEP 2
Tell it back.
Look away from the screen and tell it back in your own words, out loud, like you are telling a friend what it said. Out of order is fine. Casual is fine. Laughing is fine.
COLOUR IT
“Who counted that?”
STEP 3
Colour it.
React. A doubt, a question, an example, a joke, a feeling. One or two seconds is plenty. It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours.
space
STEP 4
The Recall.
Press space. The stimulus disappears and does not come back. Now say the entire argument back from memory, out loud, with every detail you can still hold. This is the part that gets scored.
This step
Full instructions
STEP 1 OF 4
Read it.

Read about one sentence at a time, silently. Give each word enough attention to hear it naturally in your head. If that feels like too much to hold, take a shorter piece. When you take on too much, the part that slips is always the beginning.

Next step
Your own words, every detail.

Three ways to say the same stimulus back. Only one of them is the drill.

RETELLING

“The planners want to widen the road, but the last three cities that tried it got a fifth more traffic in two years, so it will probably backfire.”

Own words. Every detail. Held together as an argument. This is the drill.

RECITING

“City planners argue that widening Elm Street will ease congestion. But in the three cities that widened comparable roads…”

## assistant

Fixed and shipped. Both accounts are clean.

## The real bug I found

Yahunathan's 8 sections were assigned as individual rows, each with its own message and classroom link. The library treated every one of them as a *run he'd abandoned*: it printed "Attempt 1 · not finished" for sections he'd never opened, offered "Run it again", and pressing that called `start_section_attempt` — which **deletes** a never-started row for that same section before inserting its own.

So the first time he went through the library door, the row his inbox link pointed at would vanish mid-session and the link would answer "Activity not found." Same silent shape as Ivory's flag: the product had no opinion about whether what it had just written to his account was still reachable.

**Fix:** a row he has never opened is not an Attempt, it *is* the assignment. The library now skips those when listing runs, so the button reads "Start", and pressing it opens that row instead of asking the server for a new one. A run he genuinely started and walked away from is untouched — half a run has no valid audio to resume into (ADR 0023), so it still goes through `start_section_attempt`.

Verified three ways: 5 new tests red→green, then the whole suite (98 files / 2,699 tests) and typecheck clean, then driven in a real browser on the preview harness — PT1.S3 renders "Not run yet." / "Start", and clicking it logged `[preview] start r4`, the assigned row itself, not a server-made replacement.

Commits `e60b40a` and `ce70f95`.

## It was pushed but not live — so I published it

Your push made Lovable *build* the commit (its preview screenshot was tagged `e60b40a`), but lsatprep.study kept serving the old bundle. Publishing is a separate call. I checked how far prod was behind first: it already had `7ffd263` from 13:56, so publishing shipped only `b9f00d7`, the scripts-only `15a5a2b`, my fix, and a dev-only harness file — a small surface, not four hours of other sessions' work.

Proven live, not assumed: prod moved from `index-BN9kDi6r.js` to `index-C0-FpvAm.js`, and the `started_at` marker that only my change introduces is now in `SectionLibraryPanel-C58psbTy.js`.

## Then I gave Yahunathan the door

`Assigned. id=517c517b… · inbox ok · bell ok · email ok`. He now matches Ore, Charles and Celia. I deliberately did this *after* the deploy — handing him the door before the fix was live would have walked him straight into the delete.

## Two things I did not change, deliberately

**`unlock_at` is a label, not a lock, outside the classroom.** Their sections are staggered — Yahunathan gets two a day through Sep 11, Ore one a day — and the classroom weekly view correctly disables the locked ones. But `/activities` doesn't consult `unlock_at` at all, so all 8 are one click away right now. Nothing breaks if he runs ahead; it just means the schedule is advisory. Enforcing it is a product decision, and an hour before his session is the wrong time for me to make it.

**The sweeper is still unscheduled.** If tonight's grade is refused or dies, nothing tells you. After his session:

```bash
cd C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu && npx vite-node scripts/rrc-ungraded.ts
```
